F1: Git percent-decodes the SSH authority before splitting user and host, so `ssh://evil.com%2f@github.com/owner/repo` (also `%2F`, `%0a`, and `.git` forms) reaches the host `evil.com`, yet both resolvers match the raw text as github.com and run `secret set`, `workflow run`, and `secret delete` against `owner/repo`; curl keeps github.com as the host for the HTTPS form, so only SSH needs the percent-escape rejection. The reviewer's correction splits the scheme case (HTTP(S) keeps credentialed userinfo, `ssh://` rejects `%` in userinfo, `git://` keeps the bare host requirement) and adds three negatives. F2: the body's "a clone suffix or dot-leading owner simply reaches `gh` as a harmless 404" sentence is false for dot-leading overrides (they warn and fall back to a recognized origin), and the ledger's "no accepted origin had a non-github.com host" line is disproved by F1; both must be corrected and the negative count refreshed. The review workflow forwarded `/oc lab` at 04:52:58Z; Lab Engineer `41580662092` is live on that head since 04:53:03Z.
